Now, let's think about the DP state and first_gt/first_perfect with pos as previous cut index. We use 0-based gap indices. Initial pos=-1. first_gt(-1,D) should search from index0. AtCoder max_right with l=0 works. If pos=G-1, l=G, return G.

Segment tree max_right implementation details:

```
class SegTree:
    def __init__(self, arr):
        self.n = len(arr)
        self.size = 1
        while self.size < self.n:
            self.size <<= 1
        self.data = [-1] * (2*self.size)
        for i, v in enumerate(arr):
            self.data[self.size+i] = v
        for i in range(self.size-1, 0, -1):
            self.data[i] = max(self.data[2*i], self.data[2*i+1])
    def first_ge(self, l, x):
        if l >= self.n: return self.n
        l += self.size
        sm = -1
        while True:
            while (l & 1) == 0:
                l >>= 1
            # if max(sm, data[l]) >= x -> fail
            if max(sm, self.data[l]) >= x:
                while l < self.size:
                    l <<= 1
                    if max(sm, self.data[l]) < x:
                        sm = max(sm, self.data[l])
                        l += 1
                return l - self.size
            sm = max(sm, self.data[l])
            l += 1
            if (l & -l) == l:
                break
        return self.n
    def first_gt(self, l, x):
        if l >= self.n: return self.n
        l += self.size
        sm = -1
        while True:
            while (l & 1) == 0:
                l >>= 1
            if max(sm, self.data[l]) > x:
                while l < self.size:
                    l <<= 1
                    if max(sm, self.data[l]) <= x:
                        sm = max(sm, self.data[l])
                        l += 1
                return l - self.size
            sm = max(sm, self.data[l])
            l += 1
            if (l & -l) == l:
                break
        return self.n
```
Need verify AtCoder max_right loop termination. Standard uses `if (l & -l) == l: break` after increment. Let's test with l=0, size=4, n=4. l=4. while l%2==0: l=2, l=1? Wait l=4, while even: l=2 (even), l=1 (odd) stop. Then check data[1] root. If root max < x, sm=root, l=2, (2&-2)==2 break, return n. Good. If root max >= x, descend: l=1<size? size=4, l=2; check left child data[2]. If left max < x, sm=left, l=3; then loop: l=3<size, check data[3]? Wait standard descend:
```
while l < size:
    l *= 2
    if f(op(sm, d[l])):
        sm = op(sm, d[l])
        l += 1
return l - size
```
If l=1, l*=2 ->2, check left child. If left ok, sm=left, l=3. Then loop again l=3<size? size=4, l*=2 ->6, check data[6] (right child of left child). If ok, l=7, loop l=7<size false, return 7-4=3. This finds first fail. Good.

But our data leaves beyond n are -1, so root max unaffected. If l starts not 0, standard works.

Need be careful with `sm` initial -1. Gaps positive, so -1 identity.

Now, perfect_dict. We need G_pref parity for each gap index. We can compute while building g:
```
pref = 0
perfect_dict = {}
for i, val in enumerate(g):
    pref ^= (val & 1)  # since sum mod2 xor
    key = pref ^ (val & 1)
    if val not in perfect_dict: perfect_dict[val] = [[], []]
    perfect_dict[val][key].append(i)
```
Wait pref is G_pref[i] = sum_{0..i} g mod2. We update pref ^= val&1. key = pref ^ (val&1). For i0 g=1: pref=1, key=0. Good. For i1 g=2: pref=1^0=1, key=1^0=1. Good.

But note G_pref parity is sum of g up to i. If we need G_r for edge r (1-based) = sum_{1..r} g. 0-based i = r-1, so G_pref[i] = sum_{0..i} g = G_r. Correct.

if lst is None: return INF
arr = lst[target_key]
idx = bisect_right(arr, pos)
return arr[idx] if idx < len(arr) else INF
```

DP function:
```
def exists_dp(Q):
    dp0 = -1
    dp1 = INF
    for D in Ds:
        ndp0 = ndp1 = INF
        # state 0
        if dp0 != INF:
            r1 = seg.first_gt(dp0, D)
            r2 = first_perfect(dp0, D, 0 ^ Q)
            r = r1 if r1 < r2 else r2
            if r < G:
                if D & 1:
                    ndp1 = r if r < ndp1 else ndp1
                else:
                    ndp0 = r if r < ndp0 else ndp0
        # state 1
        if dp1 != INF:
            r1 = seg.first_gt(dp1, D)
            r2 = first_perfect(dp1, D, 1 ^ Q)
            r = r1 if r1 < r2 else r2
            if r < G:
                if (D & 1) ^ 1:
                    ndp0 = r if r < ndp0 else ndp0
                else:
                    ndp1 = r if r < ndp1 else ndp1
        dp0, dp1 = ndp0, ndp1
        if dp0 == INF and dp1 == INF:
            return False
    return True
```
Use INF = 10**18 or G+1. Since positions are 0..G-1, INF=G+1.

But wait: dp0 initial -1 means we have matched 0 cuts and parity0. If D&1=0, after cut parity remains0, ndp0=r. If D&1=1, ndp1=r. Good.

Now, first_gt and first_perfect both return INF if none. We can set INF = G+1.

Potential issue: In DP, if both r1 and r2 are INF, r=INF. If r < G check. Good.

    prev = r
if ok: return M0
```

    prev = r
```

Now, what about if M=1, Ds empty, property A would trivially true, answer M0. We handle separately to avoid segment tree with G=0? If K=1, G=0, M=1, segment tree not built. But if K=1 and M=1, p=[a], s=[b], L=b-a, Rsh=a-b, max=abs? max(b-a, a-b)=abs. Operation count |a-b|. Good.

So algorithm order:
- Read N, A, B.
- Extract p = [i+1 for i,ch in enumerate(A) if ch=='1']; s similarly.
- K=len(p), M=len(s).
- If K < M: print(-1); continue.
- L = s[0] - p[0]; Rsh = p[-1] - s[-1]; M0 = max(L, Rsh); R = L + Rsh.
- If R < 0: print(-1); continue. (Span condition)
- If M == 1: print(M0); continue.
- Build g, segment tree, perfect_dict.
- Ds = [s[i+1]-s[i] for i in range(M-1)]
- Check any valid ge; if fail print(-1); continue.
- Check property A strict; if success print(M0); continue.
- Determine Q_req:
   Rmod = R & 1
   if Rmod == 0:
       Q_req = 0
   else:
       if L < Rsh:
           Q_req = 1
       elif Rsh < L:
           Q_req = 0
       else:
           Q_req = -1
- If Q_req != -1 and exists_dp(Q_req): print(M0)
- else: print(M0+1)

Now, let's think about edge cases with L or Rsh negative and M0 maybe negative? Suppose R=0, L=-1,Rsh=1, M0=1. R even, Q_req=0. If property A fails? Example initial p=[2,3], B=[1,3]? s1=1,p1=2 L=-1, pK=3,sM=3 Rsh=0? R=-1? Not. Need R=0 with L=-1,Rsh=1 means s1=p1-1, sM=pK-1? Now, is first_ge greedy sufficient for any valid selection? Yes for existence with g>=D in order, greedy earliest works. Because if there is any valid sequence, choosing the earliest possible gap for the current D leaves at least as many remaining gaps for later D's. This is standard.

Is property A greedy with first_gt sufficient for all-strict existence? Same.

Is DP with earliest position per state sufficient? We need prove. State includes parity c and last cut position. For a fixed c, a smaller last cut position is always better because future allowed positions are those > last; smaller gives a superset of choices. The parity c is the only other thing affecting future perfect conditions. Thus keeping minimal position per c is sufficient. This is a standard shortest/automaton subsequence DP.

Now, let's test DP on a scenario where choosing a later strict cut for current D might allow a perfect cut for next D with required Q, while earliest strict cut would skip over that perfect? Wait if we choose earliest strict cut at position r, any later perfect cut at position p > r is still available. Choosing earlier never skips future positions; it includes all positions >r, which is a superset of >later. So earlier dominates. What if the earliest allowed cut is perfect with condition and updates c, but a later strict cut also updates c same; earlier perfect is at smaller position, dominates. Good.

Now, one subtlety: The DP state c is parity of sum of D of cuts selected so far. For a strict cut, there is no condition, but the cut's D parity still updates c. For a perfect cut, condition uses c before cut? We used c (state before cut) and Dparity to compute target key. Let's verify with sample1 if property A failed? Not needed. But let's test with δ=[0,1] example (p=[1,2,4], B={1,2,3}? Actually Ds=[1,1], Q? We simulated). For perfect cut edge1 (D=1), before cut c=0, Dparity=1, c_new=1. P_r = G_pref[0] xor c_new = 1 xor1=0. Q? For that selection Q=P_r=0. In DP Q=0, target key = c^Q =0. key[0]=0, allowed. Good. If we had used c_new in target, key = G^Dparity = c^Q, same. So correct.

Now, let's think about the derivation of Q_req for R odd with L<Rsh etc. We should re-derive carefully with signs.

Given R odd, E=1. If p_L=1 (left odd), z=1 (left endpoint op used). Then t = O + |L - A| with O=(R+1)/2, A=O-1=(R-1)/2. We derived t = max(L+1, Rsh). Let's verify algebra with L,Rsh maybe negative:
O=(L+Rsh+1)/2.
A=(L+Rsh-1)/2.
t = O + |L - A| = (R+1)/2 + | (L - Rsh +1)/2 |.
Let D=L-Rsh.
If D+1 >=0, t=(R+1 + D+1)/2 = (L+Rsh+1 + L-Rsh+1)/2 = L+1.
If D+1 <0, t=(R+1 - D -1)/2 = (R - D)/2 = (L+Rsh - (L-Rsh))/2 = Rsh.
So t = max(L+1, Rsh). Good.
If p_R=1 (right odd), z=0, O=(R+1)/2, A=O=(R+1)/2. t = O + |L - O| = max(L, Rsh+1)? Let's derive: D=L-Rsh. t=(R+1)/2 + |(L-Rsh-1)/2|. If L-Rsh-1 >=0 => t=(R+1 + L-Rsh-1)/2 = L. Else t=(R+1 - (L-Rsh-1))/2 = Rsh+1. So t=max(L, Rsh+1). Good.

Now, compare to M0=max(L,Rsh). For left odd t=max(L+1,Rsh). When is t == M0?
- If L >= Rsh: M0=L, t=L+1 (since L+1 > Rsh) -> penalty1.
- If L < Rsh: M0=Rsh, t=Rsh if Rsh > L+1? If Rsh = L+1, t=max(L+1,L+1)=L+1=M0. If Rsh > L+1, t=Rsh=M0. Since R odd and L,Rsh integers, L<Rsh implies Rsh >= L+1. Thus t=M0. So left odd penalty0 iff L < Rsh. Good.
For right odd t=max(L,Rsh+1). Penalty0 iff Rsh < L (since then M0=L, t=L if L > Rsh+1? If L=Rsh+1, t=max(Rsh+1,Rsh+1)=L=M0; if L>Rsh+1, t=L). So right odd penalty0 iff Rsh < L. Good.

If L=Rsh, R even, not in R odd.

Thus Q_req:
- If R odd and L < Rsh: need left odd -> p_L=1 -> Q=1.
- If R odd and Rsh < L: need right odd -> p_R=1 -> Q=0 (since p_R=Rmod xor Q =1 xor Q =1 => Q=0).
Good.

For R even, p_L=p_R=Q. Penalty0 iff Q=0. If Q=1, E=2 penalty1. Good.

Now, what if R even and Q=0 but there are no perfect cuts? Property A would succeed. If property A fails, any valid selection has perfect cuts with Q=0. DP Q=0 checks. If DP Q=0 succeeds, penalty0. If fails, answer M0+1. But could there be a valid selection with Q=1 and penalty1 but also some other selection with Q=0? DP would find Q=0 if exists. Good.

Now, let's consider if property A fails but DP required Q succeeds with a selection that has perfect cuts all Q, but the number of perfect cuts is zero? Impossible as argued. But to be safe, if property A fails and DP Q succeeds, we don't know if it used perfect cuts; but if it didn't, property A would have succeeded. So okay.

Let's micro-optimize segment tree query:
- Store `data`, `size`, `n` in local variables in function.
- Avoid `max(sm, data[l])` repeated by using if.
- For first_ge:
```
def first_ge(l, x):
    if l >= n: return n
    l += size
    sm = -1
    while True:
        while (l & 1) == 0:
            l >>= 1
        v = data[l]
        if sm < v: v2 = v
        else: v2 = sm
        if v2 >= x:
            while l < size:
                l <<= 1
                v = data[l]
                if sm < v: v2 = v
                else: v2 = sm
                if v2 < x:
                    sm = v2
                    l += 1
            return l - size
        if sm < v: sm = v
        l += 1
        if (l & -l) == l:
            break
    return n
```
This avoids max function call. Similar for gt.

But code complexity. We can keep max; PyPy max of two ints is not too bad. However 40 million max calls maybe okay? Maybe not. Let's write optimized but readable.

AtCoder max_right with `f` function would be slower. We'll implement two separate functions with inline condition.

Let's verify the standard loop with `sm` update. In the descend phase, when checking left child, if predicate true, we update sm and move to right child. The code:
```
while l < size:
    l <<= 1
    if predicate(op(sm, data[l])):
        sm = op(sm, data[l])
        l += 1
return l - size
```
If predicate false, we don't update sm and continue descending into left child. This is correct.

Now, let's write code.

Pseudo:

```
import sys
from bisect import bisect_right

def solve():
    input = sys.stdin.readline
    T = int(input())
    out = []
    INF = 10**18
    for _ in range(T):
        N = int(input())
        A = input().strip()
        B = input().strip()
        p = [i+1 for i,ch in enumerate(A) if ch=='1']
        s = [i+1 for i,ch in enumerate(B) if ch=='1']
        K = len(p); M = len(s)
        if K < M:
            out.append("-1")
            continue
        L = s[0] - p[0]
        Rsh = p[-1] - s[-1]
        R = L + Rsh
        if R < 0:
            out.append("-1")
            continue
        M0 = L if L >= Rsh else Rsh
        if M == 1:
            out.append(str(M0))
            continue
        # build gaps
        gaps = [p[i+1]-p[i] for i in range(K-1)]
        G = K-1
        # segment tree
        size = 1
        while size < G:
            size <<= 1
        data = [-1]*(2*size)
        for i,v in enumerate(gaps):
            data[size+i] = v
        for i in range(size-1,0,-1):
            left = data[i<<1]; right = data[i<<1|1]
            data[i] = left if left >= right else right
        # perfect dict
        perfect = {}
        pref = 0
        for i,v in enumerate(gaps):
            pref ^= (v & 1)
            key = pref ^ (v & 1)
            lst = perfect.get(v)
            if lst is None:
                lst = [[], []]
                perfect[v] = lst
            lst[key].append(i)
        del gaps
        # define first_ge, first_gt
        def first_ge(l, x):
            if l >= G:
                return G
            l += size
            sm = -1
            while True:
                while (l & 1) == 0:
                    l >>= 1
                v = data[l]
                m = sm if sm >= v else v
                if m >= x:
                    while l < size:
                        l <<= 1
                        v = data[l]
                        m = sm if sm >= v else v
                        if m < x:
                            sm = m
                            l += 1
                    return l - size
                if v > sm:
                    sm = v
                l += 1
                if (l & -l) == l:
                    break
            return G
        def first_gt(l, x):
            if l >= G:
                return G
            l += size
            sm = -1
            while True:
                while (l & 1) == 0:
                    l >>= 1
                v = data[l]
                m = sm if sm >= v else v
                if m > x:
                    while l < size:
                        l <<= 1
                        v = data[l]
                        m = sm if sm >= v else v
                        if m <= x:
                            sm = m
                            l += 1
                    return l - size
                if v > sm:
                    sm = v
                l += 1
                if (l & -l) == l:
                    break
            return G
        Ds = [s[i+1]-s[i] for i in range(M-1)]
        # any valid ge
        prev = -1
        ok = True
        for D in Ds:
            r = first_ge(prev, D)
            if r >= G:
                ok = False; break
            prev = r
        if not ok:
            out.append("-1")
            continue
        # property A strict
        prev = -1
        ok = True
        for D in Ds:
            r = first_gt(prev, D)
            if r >= G:
                ok = False; break
            prev = r
        if ok:
            out.append(str(M0))
            continue
        # determine Q_req
        Rmod = R & 1
        if Rmod == 0:
            Q_req = 0
        else:
            if L < Rsh:
                Q_req = 1
            elif Rsh < L:
                Q_req = 0
            else:
                Q_req = -1
        def first_perfect(pos, D, target):
            lst = perfect.get(D)
            if lst is None:
                return G
            arr = lst[target]
            idx = bisect_right(arr, pos)
            if idx < len(arr):
                return arr[idx]
            return G
        def exists_dp(Q):
            dp0 = -1
            dp1 = G + 1  # INF
            for D in Ds:
                ndp0 = G + 1
                ndp1 = G + 1
                # state 0
                if dp0 <= G:
                    r1 = first_gt(dp0, D)
                    r2 = first_perfect(dp0, D, 0 ^ Q)
                    r = r1 if r1 < r2 else r2
                    if r < G:
                        if D & 1:
                            if r < ndp1: ndp1 = r
                        else:
                            if r < ndp0: ndp0 = r
                # state 1
                if dp1 <= G:
                    r1 = first_gt(dp1, D)
                    r2 = first_perfect(dp1, D, 1 ^ Q)
                    r = r1 if r1 < r2 else r2
                    if r < G:
                        if (D & 1) ^ 1:
                            if r < ndp0: ndp0 = r
                        else:
                            if r < ndp1: ndp1 = r
                dp0, dp1 = ndp0, ndp1
                if dp0 > G and dp1 > G:
                    return False
            return True
        if Q_req != -1 and exists_dp(Q_req):
            out.append(str(M0))
        else:
            out.append(str(M0 + 1))
    print("\n".join(out))
```

Need be careful: In exists_dp, `dp0 = -1` means finite. `dp1 = G+1` INF. Condition `if dp0 <= G` works for -1. After update, ndp values are positions 0..G-1 or G+1. Good.

But in first_ge/gt, if l = -1, l >= G false, l += size gives size-1? Wait l=-1 + size = size-1, not 0! We need handle l=-1 as search from index0. In functions, if l < 0, set l=0. In greedy and DP, prev initial -1. So first_ge(-1,D) should search from index0. Our code `if l >= G` false, `l += size` -> size-1, wrong. Need set `if l < 0: l = 0` before l += size. Or initialize prev = -1 and in functions handle. Add:
```
if l < 0: l = 0
if l >= G: return G
```
Similarly first_gt. In DP, dp0=-1, dp1 maybe -1? dp1 never -1. So add.

Alternatively initialize prev = -1 and pass; functions should handle.

Also in first_perfect, bisect_right(arr, pos) with pos=-1 works (returns0). Good.

Now, perfect_dict building: pref parity of sum gaps up to i. We used `pref ^= (v & 1)`. Since sum mod2 is xor of parities. key = pref ^ (v & 1). Let's verify with g=[1,2,3]:
i0 v1 pref1 key0.
i1 v2 pref1 key1.
i2 v3 pref0 (1^1=0? 1^1=0) key=0^1=1. Earlier I computed G_pref i2=6 mod2=0, key=1. Good.

Now, in DP, for perfect cut condition, target_key = c ^ Q. But key = G_pref ^ Dparity. We derived c = key ^ Q? Let's re-derive with 0-based:
P_r = G_pref[i] xor c_new (where c_new = c ^ Dparity). Need P_r = Q.
So G_pref xor c ^ Dparity = Q => G_pref xor Dparity = c ^ Q. key = G_pref xor Dparity. So target_key = c ^ Q. Correct.

Now, one more subtlety: In the gap reduction model, a strict cut (g_r > D_m) has δ_r = g_r - D_m >0. It is part of a positive component. A perfect cut (g_r = D_m) has δ_r=0 and splits components. Our DP treats strict cuts as no condition, perfect cuts as condition. But what about a cut with g_r > D_m but δ_r parity and prefix parity? It contributes to P and affects future perfect cuts' Q. Our DP state c tracks sum of D parities, and first_perfect condition uses G_pref and c. Strict cuts update c by Dparity, so P_r changes accordingly. Thus future perfect cuts see correct P. Good.

Now, let's think about input reading. T up to2e5, sum N1e6. Use sys.stdin.buffer.read().split() for speed. We can parse tokens. Strings A,B. Let's implement fast scanner:

```
import sys
data = sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()
it = iter(data)
T = int(next(it))
for _ in range(T):
    N = int(next(it))
    A = next(it).decode()
    B = next(it).decode()
...
```
But decoding strings to iterate chars; bytes iteration yields ints, faster. We can extract positions from bytes:
```
A = next(it)  # bytes
p = [i+1 for i,ch in enumerate(A) if ch == 49]  # ord('1')=49
B = next(it)
s = [i+1 for i,ch in enumerate(B) if ch == 49]
```
This avoids decode. N is given but not needed except maybe. Use bytes.

```

Can an operation reduce edges on both sides of a zero δ edge? That would require targeting a piece at the zero edge? Let's examine: Suppose edge e has δ_e=0 (no reduction needed). So no block spanning it. Thus operations cannot cross a zero δ edge. Our component model is correct: positive components separated by zero δ edges are independent. Good.

Let's write final code carefully:

```
import sys
from bisect import bisect_right

def solve():
    data = sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()
    it = iter(data)
    T = int(next(it))
    out = []
    for _ in range(T):
        N = int(next(it))
        A = next(it)
        B = next(it)
        p = [i+1 for i,ch in enumerate(A) if ch == 49]
        s = [i+1 for i,ch in enumerate(B) if ch == 49]
        K = len(p); M = len(s)
        if K < M:
            out.append("-1")
            continue
        L = s[0] - p[0]
        Rsh = p[-1] - s[-1]
        R = L + Rsh
        if R < 0:
            out.append("-1")
            continue
        M0 = L if L >= Rsh else Rsh
        if M == 1:
            out.append(str(M0))
            continue
        gaps = [p[i+1] - p[i] for i in range(K-1)]
        G = K - 1
        size = 1
        while size < G:
            size <<= 1
        seg = [-1] * (2 * size)
        for i, v in enumerate(gaps):
            seg[size + i] = v
        for i in range(size - 1, 0, -1):
            a = seg[i << 1]
            b = seg[i << 1 | 1]
            seg[i] = a if a >= b else b

        perfect = {}
        pref = 0
        for i, v in enumerate(gaps):
            pref ^= (v & 1)
            key = pref ^ (v & 1)
            lst = perfect.get(v)
            if lst is None:
                lst = [[], []]
                perfect[v] = lst
            lst[key].append(i)
        del gaps

        def first_ge(pos, x):
            l = pos + 1
            if l < 0:
                l = 0
            if l >= G:
                return G
            l += size
            sm = -1
            while True:
                while (l & 1) == 0:
                    l >>= 1
                v = seg[l]
                m = sm if sm >= v else v
                if m >= x:
                    while l < size:
                        l <<= 1
                        v = seg[l]
                        m = sm if sm >= v else v
                        if m < x:
                            sm = m
                            l += 1
                    return l - size
                if v > sm:
                    sm = v
                l += 1
                if (l & -l) == l:
                    break
            return G

        def first_gt(pos, x):
            l = pos + 1
            if l < 0:
                l = 0
            if l >= G:
                return G
            l += size
            sm = -1
            while True:
                while (l & 1) == 0:
                    l >>= 1
                v = seg[l]
                m = sm if sm >= v else v
                if m > x:
                    while l < size:
                        l <<= 1
                        v = seg[l]
                        m = sm if sm >= v else v
                        if m <= x:
                            sm = m
                            l += 1
                    return l - size
                if v > sm:
                    sm = v
                l += 1
                if (l & -l) == l:
                    break
            return G

        Ds = [s[i+1] - s[i] for i in range(M-1)]

        prev = -1
        ok = True
        for D in Ds:
            r = first_ge(prev, D)
            if r >= G:
                ok = False
                break
            prev = r
        if not ok:
            out.append("-1")
            continue

        prev = -1
        ok = True
        for D in Ds:
            r = first_gt(prev, D)
            if r >= G:
                ok = False
                break
            prev = r
        if ok:
            out.append(str(M0))
            continue

        def first_perfect(pos, D, target):
            lst = perfect.get(D)
            if lst is None:
                return G
            arr = lst[target]
            idx = bisect_right(arr, pos)
            if idx < len(arr):
                return arr[idx]
            return G

        def exists_dp(Q):
            dp0 = -1
            dp1 = G + 1
            for D in Ds:
                ndp0 = G + 1
                ndp1 = G + 1
                if dp0 <= G:
                    r1 = first_gt(dp0, D)
                    r2 = first_perfect(dp0, D, 0 ^ Q)
                    r = r1 if r1 < r2 else r2
                    if r < G:
                        if D & 1:
                            if r < ndp1:
                                ndp1 = r
                        else:
                            if r < ndp0:
                                ndp0 = r
                if dp1 <= G:
                    r1 = first_gt(dp1, D)
                    r2 = first_perfect(dp1, D, 1 ^ Q)
                    r = r1 if r1 < r2 else r2
                    if r < G:
                        if (D & 1) ^ 1:
                            if r < ndp0:
                                ndp0 = r
                        else:
                            if r < ndp1:
                                ndp1 = r
                dp0, dp1 = ndp0, ndp1
                if dp0 > G and dp1 > G:
                    return False
            return True

        Rmod = R & 1
        if Rmod == 0:
            Q_req = 0
        else:
            if L < Rsh:
                Q_req = 1
            elif Rsh < L:
                Q_req = 0
            else:
                Q_req = -1

        if Q_req == -1:
            if exists_dp(0) or exists_dp(1):
                out.append(str(M0 + 1))
            else:
                out.append("-1")
        else:
            if exists_dp(Q_req):
                out.append(str(M0))
            elif exists_dp(1 - Q_req):
                out.append(str(M0 + 1))
            else:
                out.append("-1")
    sys.stdout.write("\n".join(out))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    solve()
```

    if r < ndp1: ndp1 = r
```

Good catch.

Let's now produce final code with the corrected c=1 block.</think>```python
import sys
from bisect import bisect_right


def solve():
    data = sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()
    it = iter(data)
    T = int(next(it))
    out = []

    for _ in range(T):
        N = int(next(it))
        A = next(it)
        B = next(it)

        p = [i + 1 for i, ch in enumerate(A) if ch == 49]
        s = [i + 1 for i, ch in enumerate(B) if ch == 49]

        K = len(p)
        M = len(s)

        if K < M:
            out.append("-1")
            continue

        L = s[0] - p[0]
        Rsh = p[-1] - s[-1]
        R = L + Rsh

        if R < 0:
            out.append("-1")
            continue

        M0 = L if L >= Rsh else Rsh

        if M == 1:
            out.append(str(M0))
            continue

        gaps = [p[i + 1] - p[i] for i in range(K - 1)]
        G = K - 1

        size = 1
        while size < G:
            size <<= 1

        seg = [-1] * (2 * size)
        for i, v in enumerate(gaps):
            seg[size + i] = v
        for i in range(size - 1, 0, -1):
            a = seg[i << 1]
            b = seg[i << 1 | 1]
            seg[i] = a if a >= b else b

        perfect = {}
        pref = 0
        for i, v in enumerate(gaps):
            pref ^= (v & 1)
            key = pref ^ (v & 1)
            lst = perfect.get(v)
            if lst is None:
                lst = [[], []]
                perfect[v] = lst
            lst[key].append(i)

        del gaps

        def first_ge(pos, x):
            l = pos + 1
            if l < 0:
                l = 0
            if l >= G:
                return G
            l += size
            sm = -1
            while True:
                while (l & 1) == 0:
                    l >>= 1
                v = seg[l]
                m = sm if sm >= v else v
                if m >= x:
                    while l < size:
                        l <<= 1
                        v = seg[l]
                        m = sm if sm >= v else v
                        if m < x:
                            sm = m
                            l += 1
                    return l - size
                if v > sm:
                    sm = v
                l += 1
                if (l & -l) == l:
                    break
            return G

        def first_gt(pos, x):
            l = pos + 1
            if l < 0:
                l = 0
            if l >= G:
                return G
            l += size
            sm = -1
            while True:
                while (l & 1) == 0:
                    l >>= 1
                v = seg[l]
                m = sm if sm >= v else v
                if m > x:
                    while l < size:
                        l <<= 1
                        v = seg[l]
                        m = sm if sm >= v else v
                        if m <= x:
                            sm = m
                            l += 1
                    return l - size
                if v > sm:
                    sm = v
                l += 1
                if (l & -l) == l:
                    break
            return G

        Ds = [s[i + 1] - s[i] for i in range(M - 1)]

        # Check whether any valid cut selection exists.
        prev = -1
        ok = True
        for D in Ds:
            r = first_ge(prev, D)
            if r >= G:
                ok = False
                break
            prev = r

        if not ok:
            out.append("-1")
            continue

        # Check whether all cuts can be strict.
        prev = -1
        ok = True
        for D in Ds:
            r = first_gt(prev, D)
            if r >= G:
                ok = False
                break
            prev = r

        if ok:
            out.append(str(M0))
            continue

        def first_perfect(pos, D, target):
            lst = perfect.get(D)
            if lst is None:
                return G
            arr = lst[target]
            idx = bisect_right(arr, pos)
            if idx < len(arr):
                return arr[idx]
            return G

        def exists_dp(Q):
            dp0 = -1
            dp1 = G + 1

            for D in Ds:
                ndp0 = G + 1
                ndp1 = G + 1

                if dp0 <= G:
                    r1 = first_gt(dp0, D)
                    r2 = first_perfect(dp0, D, 0 ^ Q)
                    r = r1 if r1 < r2 else r2
                    if r < G:
                        if D & 1:
                            if r < ndp1:
                                ndp1 = r
                        else:
                            if r < ndp0:
                                ndp0 = r

                if dp1 <= G:
                    r1 = first_gt(dp1, D)
                    r2 = first_perfect(dp1, D, 1 ^ Q)
                    r = r1 if r1 < r2 else r2
                    if r < G:
                        if D & 1:
                            if r < ndp0:
                                ndp0 = r
                        else:
                            if r < ndp1:
                                ndp1 = r

                dp0, dp1 = ndp0, ndp1
                if dp0 > G and dp1 > G:
                    return False

            return True

        Rmod = R & 1

        if Rmod == 0:
            Q_req = 0
        else:
            if L < Rsh:
                Q_req = 1
            elif Rsh < L:
                Q_req = 0
            else:
                Q_req = -1

        if Q_req == -1:
            if exists_dp(0) or exists_dp(1):
                out.append(str(M0 + 1))
            else:
                out.append("-1")
        else:
            if exists_dp(Q_req):
                out.append(str(M0))
            elif exists_dp(1 - Q_req):
                out.append(str(M0 + 1))
            else:
                out.append("-1")

    sys.stdout.write("\n".join(out))


if __name__ == "__main__":
    solve()
```
